A regal manor house in the garden district of a major city, Lurewood Mansion is the conclave's academy. The previous owners donated their property generations ago for unknown reasons. The manor's architecture has been adapted to include numerous salons and studies, as well as a fine kitchen.

In the studies, members of the Bejeweled Conclave learn to negotiate deals and defuse arguments. In the salons, singers, dancers, and musicians practice entertainment suitable for elevated private parties, from subdued chamber music to sensual dances to heart-wrenching ballads. New faction members take lessons here no matter how polished they are when recruited, if for no other reason than to bolster the illusion that negotiation and entertainment are the Bejeweled Conclave's prime objectives.

Beneath the mansion, a dizzying maze of chambers connected through locked doors and one-way mirrors facilitates practice in eavesdropping, lock-picking, quick changes, and stealthy escapes. Other rooms allow members to rest out of sight. The heart of the maze contains the mansion's best-kept secret: workrooms where enchanters teach apprentice mages the arts of twisting minds, magically hiding secrets, and erasing memories.

#### Joining the Conclave

Aspiring faction members can't join the Bejeweled Conclave by requesting admission; members inevitably deny that there are any openings in what they claim is simply a loose professional organization.

Entry is only by invitation. Senior members carefully watch entertainers and mediators who have impressive public reputations. Those who seem particularly shrewd and ambitious might receive an invitation for free training in their specialties at Lurewood Mansion, hosted by Madame Ilverne.

Once an invitee arrives, Madame Ilverne engages them in cryptic conversation. If the potential member impresses her with their poise, acumen, and discretion, she invites them to join the faction. Otherwise, she thanks them for their time and sends them on their way without ever indicating that the mansion is anything other than a professional academy.

### Renown Rewards

Characters gain renown with the Bejeweled Conclave by sharing valuable secrets or completing conclave missions. As their Renown Scores increase, they gain access to various perks, as shown on the Bejeweled Conclave Renown table. If your campaign doesn't use renown, the DM decides if and when characters earn these perks.

##### Bejeweled Conclave Renown
| Renown Score | Reward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
|:------------:|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|      3+      | You can consult the Bejeweled Conclave before meeting a significant figure for the first time to learn a personal secret—humorous, embarrassing, or otherwise socially relevant—about that person.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
|      5+      | You can stay in Lurewood Mansion or any other residence operated by the conclave, providing you a *Modest* lifestyle for free. If you have just returned from a dangerous locale, conclave members insist you first take a bath.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|      10      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 10, the Bejeweled Conclave gives you one Uncommon magic item—usually a Ring or a Wondrous Item.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|      15+     | Your lifestyle at Lurewood Mansion or any conclave residence increases to *Comfortable*. Additionally, you can request the aid of 1d4 **Spies** for a mission. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
|      20+     | If you are arrested for anything short of treason in any land where the Bejeweled Conclave operates, the conclave finds a way to have your charges dismissed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|      25+     | You can arrange for a meeting with Madame Hestia Ilverne. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|      30      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 30, the conclave teaches you its entrancing magic. You always have the *Enthrall* spell prepared, and you can cast it once without expending a spell slot. You regain the ability to cast it in this way when you finish a Long Rest. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for this spell (choose when you gain this reward). You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. |
|      40+     | At your instruction, one of the conclave members casts *Modify Memory* (save DC 15) for free on a target of your choice. You must have met or seen the target at least once within the last 30 days, and the spell is cast within 24 hours of you choosing your target. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                             |
|      50+     | Members of the conclave openly share information with you. Additionally, the first time your Renown Score reaches 50, the Bejeweled Conclave imparts a significant secret about you that you didn't know. This secret is up to the DM and might take the form of a supernatural gift (see the *Dungeon Master's Guide*).                                                                                                                                                               |

### Bejeweled Conclave Characters

Joining the Bejeweled Conclave is advantageous for any character with mysterious allure, regardless of whether they use magic to enchant others. Those skilled at uncovering and trading secrets are also well suited to this faction, as are entertainers who specialize in dancing, singing, or other performance arts.

The most sought-after recruits are Bard, Rogue, Sorcerer, Warlock, or Wizard (especially those with the Enchanter subclass). Characters with proficiency in Deception, Performance, Persuasion, or Sleight of Hand or who have the Bejeweled Conclave Spy, Entertainer, or Noble background are also likely to be recruited.

#### Member Benefits

If you're a member of the Bejeweled Conclave, you receive these benefits.

***Finer Things.*** The conclave ensures its members dress well, paying you a stipend that supports your *Wealthy* lifestyle (4 GP per day).

***Trinket.*** Roll on the *Bejeweled Conclave Trinkets* table to determine what trinket you have from your time with the Bejeweled Conclave.

#### Bejeweled Conclave Trinket

Adventuring Gear

---

##### Bejeweled Conclave Trinkets
| 1d6 | Trinket                                                                 |
|:---:|-------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | An empty perfume bottle that smells faintly of longing                  |
|  2  | Three love letters in the same handwriting, signed with different names |
|  3  | A silk scarf with several hidden pockets                                |
|  4  | The signature page from a contract                                      |
|  5  | A drinking glass carved with a comet                                    |
|  6  | A gaudy engagement ring with a fake gem                                 |

### Using This Faction

As well-connected spies and secret keepers, members of the Bejeweled Conclave can provide characters with useful information. For example, the conclave might help the characters if they've lost a lead or are having trouble gaining access to an important public figure. The conclave might insist on a favor or secret in return, pulling the characters into its webs of intrigue.

>>*The style of the Bejeweled Conclave involves elegance, manners, and class: all techniques to be admired. Just beware members' hidden agendas.*— Alustriel

### Special Facility

Members of the Bejeweled Conclave can add the following special facility to their Bastions.

## Entertainment Salon

*Level 5 Bastion Facility*

- **Prerequisite:** Membership in the Bejeweled Conclave
- **Space:** Roomy  [16 sq]
- **Hirelings:** 2
- **Order:** Research

This fancy salon boasts elegant wall hangings, comfortable reclining couches, and a well-stocked bar.

### Open Pockets

Your eloquent hirelings convince others to pay a little more or to accept a little less. When you roll a die to determine how many Gold Pieces you pay or gain for a *Bastion* order or event, you can roll twice and take either result.

### Research: Learn Secrets

When you issue the *Research* order to this facility, you direct the facility's hirelings to entertain well-informed contacts and learn about a specific creature you name within 50 miles of your Bastion. After 7 days of work, the hirelings gain 1d4 facts about the named creature that aren't widely known and might be amusing, embarrassing, or useful. The hirelings also discover the creature's power relative to yours (such as whether the creature's CR is higher than, lower than, or the same as your level). At the DM's discretion, you might receive limited or incorrect information about an exceptionally private or supernaturally disguised creature.

# Bringers of Cosmic Dawn

![A member of the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn transforms a colleague into a strange creature.](img/book/AU/046-04-004.bringers-of-cosmic-dawn.webp)

A secretive organization of amoral transmuters, the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn receives abstruse directives from alien entities that hail from the dark spaces between stars. Faction members magically mutate plants and creatures in preparation for visitation by these beings—an event they believe is inevitable. They believe these entities will witness their glorious transmutations, be impressed, and bring about an exalted future full of mutated aberrations that serve transmuters' every whim.

### Goals and Activities

The exact goals of the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn are inscrutable to many members, though all believe they are helping to usher an ascended age of existence. The faction draws unprincipled transmuters, fringe scientists, and irrational astronomers to its academy and observatory, the Cosmic Oculus, where they receive fractured instructions from the faction's five leaders, known as the Starspeakers.

These communications have a consistent message: Faction members must transmute the flora and fauna surrounding the Cosmic Oculus. Invariably, this involves transforming plants into hostile, flesh-eating specimens and mutating beasts to rearrange organs, add joints to limbs, and cause them to secrete slime. In doing so, the faction hopes to gain the attention of alien entities they believe will descend and warp all worlds in their path.

The faction members are united in purpose but not in execution. Some pursue their own theories of mutation and transfiguration, experimenting on themselves as much as on other subjects. Some instructions communicated by the Starspeakers conflict with others, as though the alien entities they seek to communicate with weren't working in concert. These contradictions mean that the faction's work advances erratically, with rejected experiments as common as transformations deemed to be in concordance with the aliens' will.

#### Dawnrise Canyon

Bringers of Cosmic Dawn members carry out most transmutations far from public scrutiny. The faction is based in the secluded Dawnrise Canyon, far below the clifftop where the Cosmic Oculus stands. The once-tranquil canyon is now a dangerous valley of frightening creatures, hostile plants, aggressive transmuters, and wandering aberrations drawn to the unnatural landscape. Time moves erratically in the region thanks to frequent use of time-altering magic to speed up or slow down specific mutations.

#### Opposition to the Far Realm

While outsiders might not comprehend the distinction between the alien intelligences the Starspeakers commune with and the monsters of the Far Realm, Bringers of Cosmic Dawn members fear and reject the Far Realm as unknowable, indescribable, and inimical to life. The faction works to counter Far Realm incursions and expels any members who deal directly with that domain of incomprehensible horror.

### Leadership and Organization

The Bringers of the Cosmic Dawn lacks a rigid hierarchy, but its leaders maintain careful control over the information they receive from both members' experiments and alien intelligences.

#### Member Roles

Certain members of the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn have special roles in the faction.

***Starspeakers.*** The five enigmatic Starspeakers command the entire faction. None answer to any name besides their title, and they rarely leave the Cosmic Oculus except to measure progress within Dawnrise Canyon. Four Starspeakers (Medium **Aberrant Cultists**) are human. The fifth and most senior Starspeaker appears to be an elderly human astronomer, but she is actually a magically disguised **Aboleth** who has eschewed the Far Realm.

***Worldwarpers.*** Most faction members work at the Cosmic Oculus or Dawnrise Canyon, but others, called Worldwarpers, venture into civilized lands to engage in more far-flung experimentation. Worldwarpers seek out willing recipients to, as they put it, "gain blessings from beyond the stars."

Worldwarpers might offer to improve a banker's garden with voracious plants to deter thieves, provide an aberration to help guard a prison, or augment a diplomat's body to produce intoxicating pheromones. Like the work in Dawnrise Canyon, these puzzling changes ostensibly further a wider agenda, but Worldwarpers are rarely aware of the end goals; they merely put pieces in place as commanded and report back to the Starspeakers.

***Blots.*** The Starspeakers secretly assign faction members to find and eradicate Far Realm sympathizers. These individuals, known within the faction as Blots, also venture into places where the Far Realm's stain bleeds into the world, seeking to quash the Far Realm's presence wherever they find it.

#### Things Beyond the Stars

The Bringers of Cosmic Dawn receives messages from many alien intelligences, not all of which are aligned with one another. You can use the Alien Entities table to generate sample intelligences that are directing the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn.

>>*The multiverse is vast and full of entities you can't imagine. I judge not those who seek guidance from the stars—though if innocents come to harm, these experimenters will regret their choices.*— Alustriel

##### Alien Entities
| 1d6 | Entity                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|:---:|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | Atropus, the World Born Dead, is a malevolent entity the size of a planetoid that drifts ever closer to the world.                                                                                                   |
|  2  | Dendar, the Night Serpent, is a colossal beast that feeds on nightmares generated by its warped mind.                                                                                                                |
|  3  | Tharizdun, the Chained God, is a dark spiral of roiling nothingness. It seeks freedom from its millennia-long imprisonment and the extinction of all life.                                                           |
|  4  | Xhalovix is an amalgamation of 9,999 mutated planets preparing to ascend to godhood once it has transformed its ten-thousandth world.                                                                                |
|  5  | Trapped beneath a ruined city, Zargon the Returner seeks loyal worshippers to restore his mutation-steeped realm of slime and decadence. The dark sky reveals his malign will to those who know how to interpret it. |
|  6  | Ejected from the Negative Plane, a nameless evil entity drives mortals to despair by twisting reality into impossible forms.                                                                                         |

#### Joining the Bringers

Transmuters might seek out the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn even if they don't wish to advance the faction's goals. Faction members are undeniable masters of Transmutation magic, and some daring characters might join long enough to learn their secrets. Although some transmuters escape the faction, far too many begin to believe the Starspeakers' ramblings and become devotees—or worse, they become unwilling subjects for experimental mutations when their disloyalty is uncovered.

#### Cosmic Oculus

Once an abandoned observatory, the Cosmic Oculus became the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn's base when the faction discovered that the facility seems to magically amplify the messages of entities between the stars. In addition to the Starspeakers, faction astronomers also live in the Cosmic Oculus to record psychic signals from deep space and make nightly measurements of celestial bodies.

Most faction members never see the inside of the Cosmic Oculus. The ramshackle buildings clustered along the cliff face between the observatory and the canyon have expanded and mutated on their own, and members have used spells such as *Stone Shape* and *Fabricate* to haphazardly extend walls, enclose laboratories, and create passageways through the rock.

### Renown Rewards

Characters gain renown with the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn by engaging in aberrant transmutations or completing faction quests to recover rare ritual ingredients, samples of unusual flora and fauna, or astronomical measurements from distant locations. As their Renown Scores increase, characters gain access to various perks, as shown on the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn Renown table. If your campaign doesn't use renown, the DM decides if and when characters earn these perks.

##### Bringers of Cosmic Dawn Renown
| Renown Score | Reward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|:------------:|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|      3+      | You can freely enter Dawnrise Canyon, though you are afforded no specific protections from its dangers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
|      5+      | The Bringers of Cosmic Dawn warns you when you travel near places with Far Realm influence or a significant number of Aberrations.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|      10      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 10, the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn gives you an Uncommon magic item from the Scroll, Potion, or Wondrous Item category.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
|      15+     | 1d4 **Cultists**, convinced you embody a prophecy, become your loyal followers. You can order this retinue to assist you for one mission. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
|      20+     | You can buy Common and Uncommon Spell Scrolls that contain Transmutation spells from faction members.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
|      25+     | 1d4 **Cultist Fanatics** join your retinue of loyal followers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|      30      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 30, the faction teaches you its transmuting magic. You always have the *Alter Self* spell prepared, and you can cast it once without expending a spell slot. You regain the ability to cast it in this way when you finish a Long Rest. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for this spell (choose when you gain this reward). You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. |
|      40+     | You can buy Rare Spell Scrolls that contain Transmutation spells from faction members.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|      50+     | Faction members openly share information with you. Additionally, the first time your Renown score reaches 50, you can undertake voluntary mutations to enhance your physiology. You have Advantage on saving throws made to avoid or end the Grappled and Poisoned conditions.                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

### Bringers of Cosmic Dawn Characters

Most members of the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn are transmutation-focused mages, but any characters with an interest in astronomy, aberrations, or warping life are welcome. New recruits aren't permitted to balk at the faction's creations or goals.

The most welcome recruits are Sorcerer (especially those with the Aberrant Sorcery subclass), Warlock (especially those with the Great Old One Patron subclass), or Wizard (especially those with the Transmuter subclass). Characters with proficiency in Arcana, Medicine, or Nature or with *Navigator's Tools* are also valued as recruits, as are characters with the Cosmic Dawn Experiment, Guide, or Hermit background.

#### Member Benefits

If you're a member of the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn, you receive these benefits.

***Starwatcher Gear.*** The Bringers of Cosmic Dawn provide you with *Navigator's Tools* and a blank notebook in which to record your astronomical observations.

***Trinket.*** Roll on the *Bringers of Cosmic Dawn Trinkets* table to determine what trinket you have from your time with the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn.

#### Bringers of Cosmic Dawn Trinket

Adventuring Gear

---

##### Bringers of Cosmic Dawn Trinkets
| 1d6 | Trinket                                                        |
|:---:|----------------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | A razor-sharp, violet-tinted talon                             |
|  2  | An uneven spyglass lens that casts flickering pale-green light |
|  3  | A stone carved with stars in an unfamiliar constellation       |
|  4  | Fragments of a prophecy about flumphs                          |
|  5  | A fern frond that twitches like a tentacle                     |
|  6  | A small box containing a worm that won't die                   |

### Using This Faction

The Bringers of Cosmic Dawn can easily serve as a villainous or amoral organization in your campaign. If the characters pursue villains to the Celestial Oculus, you can determine whether the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn fight the characters with the aid of powerful Transmutation magic and strange Aberrations, or if they turn out to be curious intellectuals duped by malign influences.

However, the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn need not be villains. The characters might need the faction's extensive knowledge of Transmutation magic to undo a shape-shifting curse or restore a transmuted ally to the proper form. Members' whispers about creatures from the depths of space might presage an incursion by conquering githyanki astride dragons or a fleet of mind flayers on spelljamming vessels.

### Special Facility

Members of the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn can add the following special facility to their Bastions.

## Transmutation Vat

*Level 5 Bastion Facility*

- **Prerequisite:** Membership in the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn
- **Space:** Cramped  [4 sq] or Roomy  [16 sq]
- **Hirelings:** 1
- **Order:** Harvest

This laboratory features a vat or cauldron simmering with transformation potential.

### Harvest: Fluid Transmutation

When you issue the *Harvest* order to this facility, you instruct your hireling to pour a substance into the vat and transform it into something else. You must expend a Common or Uncommon potion, which is destroyed in the vat. At the end of 7 days, the hireling produces your choice of a flask of *Alchemist's Fire*, a vial of *Antitoxin*, a vial of *Basic Poison*, or a Common or Uncommon potion of your choice.

### Enlarging the Facility

You can enlarge your Transmutation Vat into a Roomy facility by spending 2,000 GP. When you issue the *Harvest* order to a Roomy Transmutation Vat, you can expend two Common or Uncommon potions. If you do, your hireling produces two substances of your choice instead of one; the options can be different.

# Covenant of the Grave

The Covenant of the Grave is a loose affiliation of necromancers and those interested in studying death and undeath through a disciplined, scientific lens. The covenant has no room for megalomaniacs who seek domination via hordes of undead minions.

These realists are aware that their studies, no matter how clinical, unnerve most people, so their members are unobtrusive and their laboratories hidden. The greatest covenant academy is the Pale Tower, a morbid, bone-white structure far from civilization.

### Goals and Activities

Covenant members are typically more interested in deepening their knowledge than their power. They freely share their research with each other via correspondence, personal visits, or clandestine lectures, but they rarely reveal discoveries to those outside the organization. Many worry that their research might be misused for sinister, nonacademic ends. Others simply don't expect anyone but like-minded scholars to comprehend their work.

Unlike necromancers who incite undead uprisings, the Covenant of the Grave usually works to stymie undead hordes and return less powerful undead to the safer confines of the laboratory or grave. Members consider undead rampages dreadfully unscientific, if not outright embarrassing. They're keenly aware of the harm that such villainous necromancy brings to the faction's reputation. Their opposition to necromantic wickedness sometimes puts the faction in conflict with powerful undead such as liches, death knights, or vampires.

### Leadership and Organization

Although an established hierarchy exists within the Covenant of the Grave, members are usually too engrossed in their own research to worry about rank.

#### Necrodemics and Gravetenders

Any faction member who shares a significant scholarly truth about death, decay, or undeath with the faction might gain the title of Necrodemic. Some members hold expanded titles such as Arisen Necrodemic or Necrodemic Embalmist, awarded for sharing more complex knowledge.

The title of Gravetender is held by the three head administrators of the Pale Tower. Emeritus administrators keep this title after retirement, so four to six Gravetenders usually exist at a time. Gravetenders set the overall direction for the faction but are usually bookish and circumspect, so change within the faction is gradual.

#### Joining the Covenant

![Members of the Covenant of the Grave show respect to undead individuals who allow themselves to be studied.](img/book/AU/047-04-005.covenant-of-the-grave.webp)

Any spellcaster with an interest in Necromancy magic or macabre research is likely to hear about the Covenant of the Grave, but the faction doesn't actively recruit. The covenant expects aspirants to independently identify its members and present a well-reasoned proposal for membership. A member who receives a qualified aspirant usually confers with other members before either taking on the aspirant as a research assistant or sending them to study at the Pale Tower.

#### Undead Members

Only a few members of the Covenant of the Grave are undead. Covenant members have little use for less powerful undead such as skeletons and zombies, and other undead are usually too consumed by their hungers to maintain the intellectual rigor the covenant expects. The Undead Members table details the few undead scholars in the covenant.

##### Undead Members
| Name              | Description                                                                                                                                                              |
|-------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Adlin Humbington  | A bookish **Ghost** who haunts his surgical theater, Adlin is so tied to his experiments that he's unaware he's dead.                                                    |
| Kadra Faldrig     | The Lawful Neutral **Lich** Kadra hides that she's undead so her highly regarded insights into lichdom seem inspired by intellect rather than a study of her own nature. |
| Melodin the Risen | A determined **Revenant**, Melodin is a Gravetender of the Pale Tower who seeks to avenge herself against her predecessor by proving herself the superior administrator. |

#### The Pale Tower

The greatest concentration of covenant members resides in the Pale Tower. Rising from a noxious swamp, this bone-white structure is thousands of years old. The tower's small, dingy exterior belies its underground warren of classrooms, laboratories, and ossuaries.

In addition to being an academy and research institution, the Pale Tower is a repository of sinister necromantic magic that the covenant doesn't want misused. Rumors hold that malicious artifacts, lost Necromancy spells, and soul-staining magic items are locked away in vaults only the Gravetenders can access.

### Renown Rewards

Characters gain renown with the Covenant of the Grave by sharing scholarly insights into death or undeath or by completing quests for the Covenant. As their Renown Scores increase, characters gain access to various perks, as shown on the Covenant of the Grave Renown table. If your campaign doesn't use renown, the DM decides if and when characters earn these perks.

##### Covenant of the Grave Renown
| Renown Score | Reward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|:------------:|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|      3+      | You receive free lodging at the Pale Tower and can access its extensive subterranean libraries. The covenant safely contains or destroys malevolent magic items you surrender to its care.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
|      5+      | You can purchase the costly Material components required for Necromancy spells you can cast from covenant members.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
|      10      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 10, the covenant gives you an Uncommon magic item from the Rod or Wand category.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|      15+     | Covenant members freely cast the *Remove Curse* spell on you and your allies.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|      20+     | You earn the honorary title of Necrodemic. When you surrender a magic item to the covenant, the covenant gifts you a different magic item of an equal or lesser rarity from the same category as the surrendered item.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|      25+     | You can arrange for a meeting with any of the faction's Gravetenders. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
|      30      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 30, the faction shares necromantic secrets with you. You always have the *Speak with Dead* spell prepared, and you can cast it once without expending a spell slot. You regain the ability to cast it in this way when you finish a Long Rest. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for this spell (choose when you gain this reward). You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. |
|      40+     | You earn the honorary title of Arisen Necrodemic. You can request the help of 1d4 **Mages** for one mission. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
|      50+     | If you or one of your allies dies and the body is brought to the Pale Tower, a covenant member casts a *Resurrection* spell, targeting the deceased. This reward is free the first time you use it. On subsequent occasions, you must provide any Material components normally consumed by the spell.                                                                                                                                                                                           |

### Covenant of the Grave Characters

Joining the Covenant of the Grave is advantageous to any character interested in the secrets of life and death. While intellectual rigor is important, knowledge of any death-adjacent subject is sufficient for aspiring members.

The most welcome aspirants are Cleric, Monk, and Wizard (especially those with the Necromancer subclass). Characters with proficiency in Arcana, Investigation, or Medicine; proficiency with *Leatherworker's Tools*; or the Covenant of the Grave Recruit, Sage, or Scribe background are also good fits.

#### Member Benefits

If you're a member of the Covenant of the Grave, you receive these benefits.

***Grave Services.*** The covenant respectfully performs final rites for your corpse whenever the inevitable comes to pass.

***Modest Lifestyle.*** The covenant pays you a stipend that supports your *Modest* lifestyle (1 GP per day).

***Trinket.*** Roll on the *Covenant of the Grave Trinkets* table to determine what trinket you have from your time with the covenant.

#### Covenant of the Grave Trinket

Adventuring Gear

---

##### Covenant of the Grave Trinkets
| 1d6 | Trinket                                       |
|:---:|-----------------------------------------------|
|  1  | A tin of grave dirt                           |
|  2  | A self-sterilizing trephine                   |
|  3  | A chapbook with illustrations of graveyards   |
|  4  | A zombie's twitching toe                      |
|  5  | A small wreath of dried flowers in an old box |
|  6  | A mysterious organ in a jar of amber fluid    |

### Using This Faction

Faction members might warn the characters of potential necromantic uprisings or escort the party through lands plagued with undead. Having access to graveyards and burial houses, the faction might also recover the corpse of someone the characters want to revive or question.

The covenant isn't likely to aid the characters for free. Members might request lost necromantic lore in exchange for their help or task the party with defeating a necromancer or undead creature who has rejected the covenant's academic overtures.

![Two necromancers cast the {@spell Animate Dead|XPHB} spell on a nearby corpse in a graveyard.](img/book/AU/048-04-006.necromancers-casting.webp)

### Special Facility

Members of the Covenant of the Grave can add the following special facility to their Bastions.

## Morgue

*Level 5 Bastion Facility*

- **Prerequisite:** Membership in the Covenant of the Grave
- **Space:** Cramped  [4 sq] or Roomy  [16 sq]
- **Hirelings:** 1
- **Order:** Research

A morgue is a location where corpses are stored and studied. You can store up to four Small or Medium corpses in the Morgue.

### Corpse Storage

As long as this facility's hireling is present, each corpse stored in the Morgue is affected by the *Gentle Repose* spell. If the facility lacks a hireling or a corpse is removed, the facility's corpses or the removed corpse loses this protection until returned to a Morgue with a hireling.

### Research: Cadaver Insights

When you issue the *Research* order to this facility, you instruct the facility's hireling to carefully inspect the corpses. This work takes 7 days. The next time you speak with the hireling, the hireling can share the cause of death of each corpse present and answer one question you have for each corpse as though the hireling had cast the *Speak with Dead* spell on it.

### Enlarging the Facility

You can enlarge your Morgue to a Roomy facility by spending 2,000 GP. If you do so, the Morgue can store up to twelve Small or Medium corpses and up to one Large corpse.

# Crucible Keepers

The Crucible Keepers consists of daredevil evokers who venture into natural or magical disasters to study the phenomenal power at play. Some members use their Evocation magic to counter disasters and help others, while other members revel in nature's destructive forces and take inspiration from them to enhance their own devastating spellcraft.

### Goals and Activities

![Crucible Keepers love a good explosion.](img/book/AU/049-04-007.crucible-keepers.webp)

Crucible Keepers study Evocation magic to command energy and control powerful forces. They eschew solitary scholarship, preferring informal gatherings where they exchange ideas and try to impress their peers with spectacular displays of magical might.

Faction members might be thrill seekers, but they aren't fools. They plan their dangerous endeavors well in advance, spending days or weeks charting natural or magical phenomena before enjoying the rush of soaring through the eye of a hurricane or surfing a lava flow. They employ protective magic while engaging in these endeavors, and they're quick to chide fools who confront danger unprepared.

>>*Overuse of powerful evocation magic can warp the land as well as the souls of those who become drunk on magical power. The Crucible Keepers' attempt to right these wrongs is noble.*— Alustriel

### Leadership and Organization

Those drawn to the Crucible Keepers aren't the sort who like following orders. Ranks within the Crucible Keepers are unofficial, fluid, and based on frequent competitive challenges between members, with losers acknowledging winners' higher rank. Challenges usually involve magical duels, elaborate races, or dangerous stunts.

#### Joining the Crucible Keepers

The Crucible Keepers enthusiastically welcomes anyone who wants to try out for its ranks. Gaining membership is as simple as impressing any faction member with a clever display of Evocation magic, a wild physical stunt, or a dramatic parlor trick.

#### Eljar Charscale

The current head of the Crucible Keepers is a dragonborn named Eljar Charscale (Medium **Evoker Archmage**; see chapter 6). Originally a human, Eljar was executed by cruel generals when he refused to use his magic in an unpopular war. After his allies reincarnated him, Eljar fled in his new dragonborn body. He took the name Charscale on joining the Crucible Keepers.

Eljar loves the thrill of Evocation magic and is undefeated in duels of spellcraft. In addition to his magical talent, Eljar is respected for listening to everyone and offering sensible guidance. He began leading the Crucible Keepers only a few months ago after the former leader died in a forest fire.

Eljar has a strong antiwar stance that he disseminates throughout the organization. He has forbidden members of the Crucible Keepers from serving as military battle mages. Some evokers believe this stance restricts their career paths, but most understand the reasoning behind Eljar's policy.

#### Disaster Chasers

Crucible Keepers predict and study natural and magical disasters, both to warn those who will be affected and to explore nature's incredible power. You can roll on or choose a result from the Pending Disasters table to generate a sample disaster Crucible Keepers plan to address.

##### Pending Disasters
| 1d4 | Disaster                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
|:---:|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | An undersea volcano near a busy bay is showing signs of an imminent eruption. Crucible Keepers who can operate underwater rush to study it and warn those nearby of its dangers.                                                        |
|  2  | Oracles predict a terrible earthquake will devastate a metropolis in the next few weeks. Crucible Keepers race to construct magical pylons to absorb its earth-rending energy.                                                          |
|  3  | A gate to the Elemental Plane of Fire is expected to appear in remote farmland. Crucible Keepers hope to stabilize and eventually close the gate—but before closing it, they want to venture through it to study planar conflagrations. |
|  4  | An ancient white dragon that planned to bury a peaceful valley beneath an avalanche has been slain. Crucible Keepers study the weakened snowpack so they can redirect the avalanche away from the nearby villages.                      |

#### Caldera Arcadium

Although members believe that nature is the best classroom, the Crucible Keepers maintains a learning institution focused on Evocation magic. The Caldera Arcadium is a large stone building hanging over the rim of an active volcano, suspended by enormous metal chains and struts.

The temperature inside the building ranges from unpleasantly warm to blisteringly hot, but eager students fill its three classrooms every day. The building also holds magical forges for the study and creation of magic items. A stone bridge from the Arcadium's roof leads to dormitories, academic offices, and a cafeteria, all hanging off the volcano's edge.

The Caldera Arcadium's headmaster is a scholarly **Efreeti** named Nadia Flameheart, who taught magic in the City of Brass for a millennium before joining the Crucible Keepers. The only wishes she entertains are those for better grades, and her only responses are tips for studying.

Fire-blackened **Stone Golems** called the Scorchborn provide security for the Arcadium, a duty that mostly involves defusing fights between hotheaded students. The Scorchborn are also instrumental to the wards that protect the school from volcanic eruptions, but not even Nadia Flameheart understands how. Since all Scorchborn are identical, no one is sure how many there are. No danger has yet presented a sufficient threat to marshal them all in one place.

Arcadium students frequently engage in death-defying stunts they call "field trips." The most common stunt is a leap from an Arcadium balcony during an eruption, trusting an ordinary rope to prevent a fall into lava. Another popular field trip involves launching homemade hang gliders from the volcano's edge.

### Renown Rewards

Characters gain renown with the Crucible Keepers by surviving natural disasters or by completing Crucible Keeper missions. As their Renown Scores increase, characters gain access to various perks, as shown on the Crucible Keepers Renown table. If your campaign doesn't use renown, the DM decides if and when characters earn these perks.

##### Crucible Keepers Renown
| Renown Score | Reward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
|:------------:|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|      3+      | The Crucible Keepers sends you advance notice of natural disasters in your area.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|      5+      | The Crucible Keepers permits you and your companions to stay at the Caldera Arcadium.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|      10      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 10, the Crucible Keepers gives you an Uncommon magic item from the Wand or Weapon category.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|      15+     | You can buy Common and Uncommon Spell Scrolls that contain Evocation spells while at the Caldera Arcadium.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
|      20+     | A reckless **Mage Apprentice** becomes your loyal fan. You can order this apprentice to assist you for one mission. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
|      25+     | Eljar Charscale or Nadia Flameheart meets with you at your request. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
|      30      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 30, the Crucible Keepers teaches you magic to guard you from the elements. You always have the *Daylight* spell prepared, and you can cast it once without expending a spell slot. You regain the ability to cast it in this way when you finish a Long Rest. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for this spell (choose when you gain this reward). You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. |
|      40+     | Faction members openly share information with you. You can buy Rare Spell Scrolls that contain Evocation spells while at the Caldera Arcadium.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
|      50+     | You can call for an **Efreeti** to aid you with a single task; the DM determines whether the efreeti can grant a *Wish*. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

### Crucible Keepers Characters

The Crucible Keepers welcomes anyone willing to study destruction up close, which brings a high proportion of daredevils and thrill seekers into its ranks. Characters who are Barbarian, Druid, Sorcerer, or Wizard (especially those with the Evoker subclass) are likely to be sought after as members, as are those with proficiency in Intimidation, Nature, or Survival or with the Crucible Storm Chaser or Noble background.

#### Member Benefits

If you're a member of the Crucible Keepers, you receive these benefits.

***Poor Lifestyle.*** The Crucible Keepers pays you a stipend that supports a *Poor* lifestyle (2 SP per day).

***Trinket.*** Roll on the *Crucible Keepers Trinkets* table to determine what trinket you have from your time in the Crucible Keepers.

#### Crucible Keepers Trinket

Adventuring Gear

---

##### Crucible Keepers Trinkets
| 1d6 | Trinket                                           |
|:---:|---------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | A piece of metal pierced by a tornado-driven twig |
|  2  | A tattered, salt-stained doll                     |
|  3  | A chunk of volcanic glass                         |
|  4  | Broken wind chimes                                |
|  5  | A seashell blackened by a great fire              |
|  6  | Bark seared with lightning-shaped lines           |

### Using This Faction

![Members of the Crucible Keepers face each other in magical combat. It's harmless and all in good fun.](img/book/AU/050-04-008.magical-combat.webp)

Crucible Keepers are often harbingers of catastrophe. If they appear in large numbers, it's usually because they anticipate an imminent disaster. A Crucible Keeper traveling through a dangerous area might hire the party to escort them, but it would be wise to ask what awaits at the journey's end.

However, Crucible Keepers are evokers, not diviners, so their guesses are sometimes wrong. As the DM, you can decide whether that means the disaster doesn't happen, is earlier or later by days, or is some other event entirely.

Crucible Keepers also study the aftermath of disasters, both natural and magical. Their presence might be a detriment or a blessing for recovery efforts. Unscrupulous members revel in disasters' aftermath and might seek to profit from them, while selfless members work to ease suffering.

### Special Facility

Members of the Crucible Keepers can add the following special facility to their Bastions.

## Geothermal Vent

*Level 5 Bastion Facility*

- **Prerequisite:** Membership in the Crucible Keepers
- **Space:** Roomy  [16 sq]
- **Hirelings:** 1
- **Order:** Empower

A geothermal vent draws heat from deep below the surface to provide energy and power simple machines.

### Empower: Withstand Heat

When you issue the *Empower* order to this facility, for the next 7 days, the facility's hireling teaches a series of meditative techniques that allow a character to mitigate the injuries they suffer from flames. Any character who trains here for at least 8 hours on each of those days gains the following benefit at the end of the training period:

- **Heat Tolerant.** When you take Fire damage, you can take a Reaction to reduce this damage by 1d4 plus your level. This benefit lasts for 7 days.

### Hot Water

Your basic facilities benefit from a constant source of adjustable heat. For example, a dining room has a warming table to keep food hot, a kitchen has multiple ovens with varying heat, and a bedroom has a basin that warms water for washing.

# Horizon Weavers

The Horizon Weavers is a collection of far-flung conjurers, explorers, and scholars who practice summoning, teleportation, and planar travel.

Members of the Horizon Weavers have unparalleled wanderlust; they've seen sights and lived through experiences most would consider too wild to be true. However, the faction's planar sojourns have not gone unnoticed, and members now work to avert the attention of a malevolent extraplanar entity fixated on their organization.

### Goals and Activities

Horizon Weavers hone their expertise in all forms of Conjuration magic, from summoning to creation to teleportation, with a focus on extraplanar travel. Powerful Horizon Weavers create gates between planes, while junior members catalog known gates or simply wander through to see what's on the other side. New faction members usually begin by traveling their own world to experience different climates and cultures before exploring other planes.

Many faction members are also amateur or professional artisans; some weave textiles as well as magic. Horizon Weavers often incorporate spells such as *Find Familiar* or *Mage Hand* into their everyday lives, using magic for everything from companionship to household chores.

#### Horizon Weaver Cloaks

Each new member who joins the faction receives a durable cloak with their initials sewn into the collar. Horizon Weavers recognize these garments on sight and seek to retrieve any that are lost or stolen.

A Horizon Weaver's cloak repels water and protects its wearer from most types of weather. The faction replaces damaged cloaks when necessary. Some members weave the threads of former cloaks into new ones.

Many Horizon Weavers incorporate their cloaks into magic items they create. The most popular choices are *Cape of the Mountebank*, *Cloak of Protection*, and *Clothes of Mending*.

#### The Lurker

A massive beast from the liminal space between planes menaces the Horizon Weavers. This entity, known as the Lurker, towers higher than mountains, with immense pincers and a single, enormous eye that radiates malice and reflects strange lights and lines.

Several months ago, when faction members first glimpsed the Lurker, a powerful team of Horizon Weavers set out to confront the monster. The team went missing, and the Lurker has since become more aggressive, snapping at some Horizon Weavers in the split second it takes them to magically travel between planes. Sometimes this shears the unlucky traveler in half; other times, the individual never reappears. These incidents are rare, but all Horizon Weavers worry about a potential Lurker strike with each planar trip.

>>*Those who provide shelter and aid to explorers of this vast multiverse are friends to both me and Mystra.*— Alustriel

### Leadership and Organization

As befits members of a society of explorers, Horizon Weavers are widely scattered. A wandering Horizon Weaver might go weeks or months without seeing another member, and a meeting might be coincidental. Senior faction members, called Gatemakers, settle disputes or provide advice, although reaching a Gatemaker often requires a cross-planar trek.

![When necessary, members of the Horizon Weavers call on powerful allies from other planes of existence.](img/book/AU/051-04-009.horizon-weavers.webp)

#### Way Stations and Shuttles

Not all Horizon Weavers seek endless travel. Some stay in one place for months or years, perhaps with a friend or lover they met during their travels. Horizon Weavers who remain in one place are expected to maintain way stations, providing directions, supplies, and shelter to passing faction members.

Weaving slang is common in these way stations: a "shuttle" is a traveler, a "beam" is a well-established way station, a "weft" is a member with irrepressible wanderlust, and a "heddle" is someone reliable.

You can roll on the Planar Way Stations table to generate a sample way station or odd planar locale.

##### Planar Way Stations
| 1d6 | Station                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
|:---:|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | A gnome named Molly Nettle (Small **Conjurer Archmage**) keeps a gingerbread house in the *Feywild* where she welcomes Horizon Weavers but shoos away all others. She doesn't understand why visitors find her residence and her child-shaped cookie sculptures off-putting. |
|  2  | The Tidepool Larder is an air-filled grotto on the Elemental Plane of Water, stocked with salted fish and other seafood. The reclusive **Marid** caretaker, Baliob, hides from visitors and doesn't charge for supplies.                                                     |
|  3  | A dour **Githzerai Psion** named Ut maintains a peaceful monastery called Ninebells on a rocky island in Limbo. Ut lets non-Horizon Weavers stay no more than nine hours.                                                                                                    |
|  4  | Scuttlebeam is a beached ship in a Feywild forest. Its married keepers, a goliath named Rusty Jenk (Medium **Pirate Captain**) and an orc named Trellie (Medium **Mage**), don't remember how the ship got here.                                                             |
|  5  | Falling Up, a floating inn in the Elemental Plane of Air, is managed by three human, semiretired Horizon Weavers (Medium **Mages**), all eager to swap tales.                                                                                                                |
|  6  | A way station in Bytopia called the Shuttle's Rest was recently reduced to ruins by an enormous, clawed creature.                                                                                                                                                            |

#### Joining the Horizon Weavers

Any curious adventurer who longs to explore the unknown might join the faction after meeting a Horizon Weaver. An extended conversation with a member is all it takes to earn a second meeting, when the member presents the aspirant with a Horizon Weaver cloak and officially inducts them into the organization. An inducting member without weaving skills or connections might invite the new member to create their own Horizon Weaver cloak, though it must be made to the faction's specifications.

#### Spiral Conservatory

Deep in a forest on the Material Plane stands a twisting building of wood and stone that surrounds a grassy courtyard. This is the Spiral Conservatory, the first and largest Horizon Weaver way station. Here, students of Conjuration magic and planar lore learn to navigate hazardous environments, summon helpful creatures, and conjure useful equipment.

The Spiral Conservatory is located at a confluence of ley lines that provides numerous, if inconstant, gates to Arcadia, the Beastlands, and the Feywild. Fey and intelligent animals often wander through the Spiral Conservatory; some of them delight in mentoring eager Horizon Weavers.

A tiefling named Gallinaver the Heddle (Medium **Conjurer Archmage**; chapter 6) serves as the Spiral Conservatory's chief caretaker. Gallinaver and his husband, a soft-spoken **Djinni** named Cloud Lace, are the easiest Gatemakers to contact, as they're never far from the Spiral Conservatory.

### Renown Rewards

Characters gain renown with the Horizon Weavers by displaying planar knowledge, conjuring magical wonders, reporting previously unknown planar gates, or completing excursions for the faction. As their Renown Scores increase, characters gain access to various perks, as shown on the Horizon Weavers Renown table. If your campaign doesn't use renown, the DM decides if and when characters earn these perks.

##### Horizon Weavers Renown
| Renown Score | Reward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
|:------------:|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|      3+      | You can consult with the Horizon Weavers to receive general information about a plane you intend to visit.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
|      5+      | You can stay in any way station maintained by a Horizon Weaver (including the Spiral Conservatory).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|      10      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 10, the faction gives you an Uncommon magic item from the Armor or Wondrous Item category. Alternatively, if you're a member, the faction can craft your faction cloak into an Uncommon magic item of your choice.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|      15+     | You receive a 10 percent discount when buying armor, weapons, and adventuring gear from a Horizon Weaver way station.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
|      20+     | You are given one of a pair of *Sending Stones*; the other is held by a mid-level faction member. If you are lost in an extraplanar location the faction can reach, you can use the Sending Stone to call the faction for help. 1d4 **Scout Captains** then arrive to escort you to a safe destination. If your Renown Score drops below 20, your Sending Stone is confiscated.                                                                                                               |
|      25+     | The discount you receive at way stations increases to 20 percent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
|      30      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 30, the faction teaches you to traverse liminal spaces. You always have the *Misty Step* spell prepared, and you can cast it once without expending a spell slot. You regain the ability to cast it in this way when you finish a Long Rest. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for this spell (choose when you gain this reward). You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. |
|      40+     | You can arrange for a meeting with any of the faction's Gatemakers. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
|      50+     | Faction members can cast the *Plane Shift* spell at your behest, transporting you and your allies to an extraplanar location of your choice. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

### Horizon Weavers Characters

The Horizon Weavers welcome carefree, capable wanderers who yearn to see what's over the horizon or through a planar portal.

Druid, Ranger, and Wizard (especially those with the Conjurer subclass) are excellent fits. Characters with proficiency in Arcana or Survival; proficiency with *Navigator's Tools* or *Weaver's Tools*; or the Artisan, Guide, Horizon Weaver Initiate, or Wayfarer background are also welcome.

#### Member Benefits

If you're a member of the Horizon Weavers, you receive these benefits.

***Traveling Cloak.*** You receive a distinctive Horizon Weavers cloak and a set of *Traveler's Clothes*.

***Trinket.*** Roll on the *Horizon Weavers Trinkets* table to determine what trinket you have from your time with the Horizon Weavers.

#### Horizon Weavers Trinket

Adventuring Gear

---

##### Horizon Weavers Trinkets
| 1d6 | Trinket                                     |
|:---:|---------------------------------------------|
|  1  | A compass that spins lazily                 |
|  2  | A map of a lost valley                      |
|  3  | A chip of ice that never melts              |
|  4  | A ring inscribed "bring me home again"      |
|  5  | Wicker from the basket of a hot air balloon |
|  6  | Four orange leaves with unfamiliar shapes   |

### Using This Faction

The Horizon Weavers might give out quests, such as mapmaking or rescue missions, that require travel to distant places. They might also seek the adventurers' help dealing with the threat of the Lurker.

The Horizon Weavers can also rescue characters stranded in distant or inhospitable places, such as those sent to other planes by the *Prismatic Spray* or *Prismatic Wall* spell. Lost characters might be lucky enough to find a Horizon Weaver way station.

### Special Facility

Members of the Horizon Weavers can add the following special facility to their Bastions.

## Planar Gateway

*Level 17 Bastion Facility*

- **Prerequisite:** Membership in the Horizon Weavers; Ability to use an *Arcane Focus*, *Holy Symbol*, or tool as a Spellcasting Focus
- **Space:** Roomy  [16 sq]
- **Hirelings:** 1
- **Order:** Empower

The focus of this chamber is an inscribed stone archway filled with mist and strange lights. This archway can connect to other planes of existence.

### Empower: Open Gate

When you issue the *Empower* order to this facility, you instruct the facility's hireling to tune the archway to a place on another plane of existence. Tuning the archway takes 7 days, at the end of which time the gateway opens. If you provide a diamond worth 5,000+ GP as part of the tuning, the gate connects to a place you specify on another plane, as with the *Gate* spell (for transport only, not to draw a specific creature through) and destroys the diamond. If you don't provide a diamond or if a deity or another planar ruler blocks the intended gate from opening, determine the gate's destination randomly on the Random Planar Destination table. In either case, the gateway remains open for 1 minute.
