# Ninth Quill

![Members of the Ninth Quill plot the heist of magical lore.](img/book/AU/053-04-011.ninth-quill-plotting.webp)

The Ninth Quill is a faction of thieves, saboteurs, and spies who use magic to obtain and sequester powerful artifacts and knowledge by any means necessary—including robbing and sabotaging other factions. The faction's leaders consider their organization to be superior to factions focused on a specific school of magic and the safest custodian of dangerous lore and relics, but some members also steal valuables and undermine other organizations for personal gain.

### Goals and Activities

Ninth Quill members' adaptability and diverse areas of expertise help them infiltrate other groups, often posing as new recruits or long-absent members from other lands.

The Ninth Quill has a few popular catchphrases that members use as meaningful mantras—or excuses for criminality. These phrases are described in the sections that follow.

#### Our Hands Are Safest

Ninth Quill members often steal magic items and research to keep them out of others' hands. They receive extensive training in handling dangerous artifacts and resisting the influence of sentient objects. In principle, these items are sequestered in the Ninth Quill vaults (which members insist are more secure than any other faction's), but less scrupulous members might keep certain items for their own use.

#### Others Learn So We Can Earn

When the Ninth Quill steals discoveries and relics that aren't particularly harmful, it sells them to wealthy patrons to fund its operations. Desperate buyers rebuffed by other factions, such as a necromancer rejected by the Covenant of the Grave or a transmuter suspicious of the Bringers of Cosmic Dawn, might seek the services of the Ninth Quill to obtain such objects.

#### Discontent Is Our Best Defense

The Ninth Quill seeks to destabilize other factions that become too powerful. This often involves agents infiltrating a faction, then undertaking whisper campaigns, sabotaging experiments, and encouraging critical personnel to depart. The Ninth Quill agents quietly slip away once they have stoked sufficient inefficiency, paranoia, and infighting.

### Leadership and Organization

The Ninth Quill values intelligence, resourcefulness, and loyalty. A significant majority of Ninth Quill agents use spellcasting to enhance their spy craft.

#### A Careful Hierarchy

Although Ninth Quill leaders take pains to hide their identities, the faction operates under a strict hierarchal system. Agents are the lowest ranking, followed by Lieutenants, followed by Masters. Those in the highest echelon are Guild Masters.

#### Joining the Ninth Quill

The Ninth Quill operates in the shadows and therefore doesn't entertain applicants. However, the Guild Masters keep an eye on talented thieves and other morally ambiguous individuals with magical skills. If the Guild Masters believe such an individual would be an asset to the Ninth Quill, they extend an invitation to visit Blackscript College—through an anonymous courier, of course.

#### Blackscript College

The hidden academy where the Ninth Quill trains its members is located in a crime-ridden neighborhood of a busy trade city. Dilapidated warehouses, all owned by the Ninth Quill, surround the college to ensure its privacy. There's no readily apparent entrance; instead, it's accessible only by unmarked doors in twisting alleys and hidden passages.

The centuries-old college was once a legitimate magical academy, but city records show that it closed decades ago due to administrative corruption. In truth, the college simply went underground to teach magical spy craft and thievery.

A former instructor at Blackscript College's predecessor academy, the college's dean is the elf Sirethe Slipshadow (Medium **Spy Master**). Slipshadow is infamous for her ability to impersonate others and uses these skills to observe students during their studies and on early missions.

The college's library, called the Repository, holds an eclectic variety of stolen treasures. An adjacent chamber called the Ersatz Repository contains worktables where members make high-quality counterfeits of spellbooks, relics, and artifacts. Many cherished treasures held by other factions are actually Ninth Quill counterfeits.

### Renown Rewards

Characters gain renown with the Ninth Quill by completing Ninth Quill missions or by contributing stolen magical lore or magic items. As their Renown Scores increase, characters gain access to various perks, as shown on the Ninth Quill Renown table. If your campaign doesn't use renown, the DM decides if and when characters earn these perks.

##### Ninth Quill Renown
| Renown Score | Reward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
|:------------:|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|      3+      | You and your companions receive free lodging at Ninth Quill safe houses, which are present in many cities.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|      5+      | You can buy Common potions and Spell Scrolls from Ninth Quill members.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
|      10+     | If the authorities are searching for you, the Ninth Quill dispatches numerous faction members who are magically disguised to look like you. This might allow you to evade capture for days or months, at the DM's discretion. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
|      15      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 15, the Ninth Quill gives you an Uncommon magic item from the Repository.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
|      20+     | You receive a magical decoder that allows you to read written Thieves' Cant. If your Renown Score drops below 20, this decoder is confiscated.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
|      25+     | The Ninth Quill can provide you with a believable but useless duplicate of a magic item you show them. Creating the duplicate takes 1d4 days of work. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
|      30      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 30, Ninth Quill faction members teach you how to magically shroud yourself. You always have the *Nondetection* spell prepared, and you can cast it once without expending a spell slot. You regain the ability to cast it in this way when you finish a Long Rest. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for this spell (choose when you gain this reward). You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. |
|      40+     | You can buy Uncommon and Rare potions and Spell Scrolls from Ninth Quill members.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|      50      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 50, the Ninth Quill gives you a Very Rare magic item that another faction desperately wants to recover.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

### Ninth Quill Characters

The Ninth Quill uses thieves and enforcers, but the group is no mere criminal guild; its leaders prefer recruits who have magical skill or knowledge.

The most welcome are Bard, Fighter (especially those with the Eldritch Knight subclass), Rogue (especially those with the Arcane Trickster subclass), Warlock, or Wizard. Characters with proficiency in Arcana or Deception; the Agent of the Ninth Quill, Charlatan, or Criminal background; or the Magic Initiate feat are also valued.

#### Member Benefits

If you're a member of the Ninth Quill, you receive these benefits.

***Modest Lifestyle.*** The Ninth Quill pays you a stipend to support your *Modest* lifestyle (1 GP per day).

***Trinket.*** Roll on the *Ninth Quill Trinkets* table to determine what trinket you have from your time with the Ninth Quill.

#### Ninth Quill Trinket

Adventuring Gear

---

##### Ninth Quill Trinkets
| 1d6 | Trinket                                                                           |
|:---:|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | A long fake beard                                                                 |
|  2  | A fancy pen that never runs out of ink                                            |
|  3  | A bag of broken teeth                                                             |
|  4  | A scrap of paper with several crossed-out passwords and the word "caiman" circled |
|  5  | Two identical playing cards with unfamiliar suits                                 |
|  6  | A featureless mask with small eyeholes                                            |

### Using This Faction

The Ninth Quill often opposes other magical factions. You might introduce Ninth Quill **Bandits** or **Scouts** looking to steal the characters' hard-won items or lore, or you could establish intrigue-laden plots about Ninth Quill **Spies**, **Spy Masters**, or **Bandit Deceivers** who stoke rivalries and paranoia within another faction.

Alternatively, the Ninth Quill could employ or aid the characters. The faction might recruit the characters to retrieve a valuable asset or assist them with recovering and disposing of a dangerous artifact. You could even build a campaign around efforts to steal relics from each of the other magical factions.

>>*Good intentions are commonplace among members of the Ninth Quill, but never drop your guard around them.*— Alustriel

### Special Facility

Members of the Ninth Quill can add the following special facility to their Bastions.

## Heist Planning Chamber

*Level 9 Bastion Facility*

- **Prerequisite:** Membership in the Ninth Quill
- **Space:** Roomy  [16 sq] or Vast  [36 sq]
- **Hirelings:** 1
- **Order:** Research

This room has a planning table, pieces of miniature terrain to represent walls or furniture, and tomes on magical security, architecture, and spy craft useful when planning a heist.

### Research: Reconnaissance

When you issue the *Research* order to this facility, your hireling sends spies to evaluate a building within 50 miles of your Bastion. This work takes 7 days. At the end of the seventh day, the hireling provides a model of the building on the planning table. Public areas of the building are depicted accurately, and private areas are depicted only generally. Any area concealed by magic or protected by powerful guardians, at the DM's discretion, might not be depicted accurately or at all. The spies avoid all creatures, so information about the building's inhabitants is sparse.

The model remains until you use the *Research* order on this facility again, but it might become inaccurate over time if the building changes.

### Heist Preparations

A character who prepares for a theft or infiltration using the model for at least 8 hours chooses one of the following roles. The role's benefit applies while the character is within 1 mile of the building depicted by the model and lasts for 7 days or until you prepare again:

- **Enforcer:** Advantage on Charisma (Intimidation) checks
- **Face:** Advantage on Charisma (Deception) checks
- **Lookout:** Advantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks
- **Medic:** Advantage on Wisdom (Medicine) checks
- **Sneak:** Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks
- **Thief:** Advantage on Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) checks

### Enlarging the Facility

You can enlarge your Heist Planning Chamber to a Vast facility by spending 2,000 GP. If you do so, the range of Reconnaissance increases to 500 miles, and a character preparing for a heist can choose two roles, gaining the benefits of both.

# Phantasmic Circus

![Members of the Phantasmic Circus prepare for a magic-enhanced performance.](img/book/AU/054-04-012.phantasmic-circus-preparing.webp)

Part entertainers' alliance and part traveling carnival, the Phantasmic Circus is an organization of performers and illusionists who create grand spectacles and share magical techniques to delight, entertain, and misdirect.

### Goals and Activities

The Phantasmic Circus stages elaborate performances across the world, moving from city to city to bring delight and wonder. These performances are huge extravaganzas involving dozens of entertainers and drawing large audiences. The circus is renowned for illusions and pyrotechnics that reach hundreds of feet into the air.

These complex routines change with every show; Phantasmic Circus performers are always experimenting with new illusions, death-defying stunts, and musical accompaniment. Tickets and concessions are pricey, but audience members remember the faction's thrilling spectacles for the rest of their lives.

The Phantasmic Circus's performances use trained creatures for certain acts, but audience members would be shocked to realize how few genuine creatures are on display. Lifelike illusions of animals and monsters perform most stunts, while living animals are treated carefully and kept out of danger.

#### One Circus, Many Troupes

The Phantasmic Circus isn't a single traveling troupe, but rather the collective name for several groups that operate independently, each under the guidance of a manager called a Wondermaker. Circus members might shift from group to group to hone their skills, so residents of a city might never realize that the "Phantasmic Circus" that visited twice the previous year was actually two different troupes.

### Leadership and Organization

The astounding and popular Phantasmic Circus of today had humble beginnings as a small traveling carnival called the Hayver and Hayver Circus. When the Hayvers' youngest sibling, the human Della, returned to the family circus with training in Illusion magic and a gregarious gnome husband named Walnip Waddlebell, the circus started its meteoric rise.

Soon, Della and Walnip were running the circus as its ringmasters and self-appointed "First Maestros." The couple also gave the increasingly illusion-focused show a flashier name. The other Hayvers have since passed away, but the aging First Maestros (both Small **Performer Legends**), still plan spectacles, train new illusionists, and orchestrate the many troupes operating as the Phantasmic Circus.

#### Wondermakers

The First Maestros are now too old to tour, but they keep in touch with each troupe as its travels bring it back to the circus's headquarters, the Mirage Lyceum. The First Maestros appoint a Wondermaker to guide each troupe. Most Wondermakers are flashy showoffs or grand illusionists who enjoy the spotlight as ringmasters or star performers. In other troupes, the Wondermaker works behind the scenes, guiding the performers and pushing their shows to new heights.

Each Wondermaker keeps their troupe's books, protects its members, makes its travel plans, and coordinates with other Wondermakers to ensure they aren't revisiting cities too soon. They also send performers from troupe to troupe to teach gifted members additional skills or to resolve personality clashes.

#### Roustabouts

Although acrobats, illusionists, animals, and entertainers are the stars of the Phantasmic Circus, a platoon of supporters keeps the troupe running. Collectively called roustabouts, these laborers and guides let the performers take the glory while collecting reasonable pay from the troupe's Wondermaker. The visual artists who design the elaborate sets and costumes are also roustabouts; they mentor each other to hone their artistic skills.

>>*Mystra smiles on those who pluck the Weave to provide entertainment to the public. So do I—as long as that entertainment cloaks no foul deeds.*— Alustriel

#### Circus Acts

No two Phantasmic Circus performances are the same. Roll on the Phantasmic Circus Acts table to generate a sample act as a basis for other wondrous spectacles.

##### Phantasmic Circus Acts
| 1d6 | Circus Acts                                                                                                                                                     |
|:---:|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | Several Gladiators fight a beholder, which is an illusion—a hidden **Performer Legend** uses the *Major Image* spell to simulate the monster.                   |
|  2  | Six trained mammoths form a pyramid, and a giant toad hops up to the top; all the animals are realistic illusions.                                              |
|  3  | A **Performer Maestro** sings an eerie ballad about a shipwreck while **Performers** on trapezes swing over and through the illusion of a grand, doomed galley. |
|  4  | Two **Satyrs** play a compelling melody while they lead volunteers from the audience through a cage of glaring lions; the volunteers and lions are illusions.   |
|  5  | Two **Knights** joust while riding horses cloaked in illusions to look like rhinoceroses.                                                                       |
|  6  | A **Performer Maestro** navigates a wondrous illusory forest and grows 50 feet tall to step over the tallest tree at the act's conclusion.                      |

#### Joining the Phantasmic Circus

Joining the Phantasmic Circus as a performer requires rigorous tryouts under the watchful eye of a troupe's Wondermaker, who makes final decisions about applicants. Those who wish to join the circus as roustabouts, however, need only express interest and demonstrate a relevant skill to a Wondermaker, who is usually happy to accept the additional help.

#### Mirage Lyceum

The headquarters of the Phantasmic Circus, the Mirage Lyceum, was formerly the winter home of the Hayver and Hayver Circus. Once a sad collection of animal pens and bunk rooms, it is now a vibrant jumble of playhouses, amphitheaters, classrooms, and studios.

The Mirage Lyceum puts on informal performances year-round. Some performers simply want to show their skills to the First Maestros; others are junior members who aren't yet ready to take to the road. This mingling of eager pupils and wise mentors makes the Mirage Lyceum an excellent place for any illusionist to learn.

Audiences come from miles around to view these informal performances, which are cheaper than the big-city spectacles. Some wonder-struck visitors stay for long periods, and a few become inspired to join the circus life.

### Renown Rewards

Characters gain renown with the Phantasmic Circus by contributing illusory spectacles or by completing missions for the circus. As their Renown Scores increase, they gain access to various perks, as shown on the Phantasmic Circus Renown table. If your campaign doesn't use renown, the DM decides if and when characters earn these perks.

##### Phantasmic Circus Renown
| Renown Score | Reward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|:------------:|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|      3+      | You can travel with the Phantasmic Circus, receiving food and cramped lodging (a *Poor* lifestyle) while you do. You can attend performances for free.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|      5+      | After a performance, faction members freely share with you 1d6 rumors heard from the audience members.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|      10      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 10, the Phantasmic Circus gives you an Uncommon magic item from the Wand or Wondrous Item category.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
|      15+     | A troupe of 1d3 + 1 **Performers** become your loyal followers. The troupe performs simple tasks, such as restocking supplies or gathering information, but they won't willingly put themselves in danger.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|      20+     | You can buy Common and Uncommon Spell Scrolls that contain Illusion spells from faction members.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
|      25+     | Your troupe gains an additional 1d4 **Performers**. Additionally, you can ask your troupe to help you with one dangerous mission. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
|      30      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 30, circus members share their illusory tricks with you. You always have the *Mirror Image* spell prepared, and you can cast it once without expending a spell slot. You regain the ability to cast it in this way when you finish a Long Rest. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for this spell (choose when you gain this reward). You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. |
|      40+     | You can arrange for a meeting with any of the faction's Wondermakers or, when at the Mirage Lyceum, with the First Maestros. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
|      50+     | 1d4 **Performer Maestros** and an additional 1d6 **Performers** join your troupe.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

### Phantasmic Circus Characters

The Phantasmic Circus welcomes artists, entertainers, and illusionists of all types as performers, provided they impress a Wondermaker. Roustabouts can be of any class or background.

Bard, Rogue, and Wizard (especially those with the Illusionist subclass) are excellent fits, as are characters with proficiency in Performance or with the Charlatan, Entertainer, Phantasmic Circus Trouper, or Wayfarer background.

#### Member Benefits

If you're a member of the Phantasmic Circus, you receive these benefits.

***Patron Connections.*** You can always find work as an entertainer while in a settlement.

***Poor Lifestyle.*** The circus provides you with performance proceeds to support your Poor lifestyle (2 SP per day).

***Trinket.*** Roll on the *Phantasmic Circus Trinkets* table to determine what trinket you have from your time with the Phantasmic Circus.

#### Phantasmic Circus Trinket

Adventuring Gear

---

##### Phantasmic Circus Trinkets
| 1d6 | Trinket                                                                                      |
|:---:|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | An advertisement for a past circus performance, featuring your likeness                      |
|  2  | A small tin of white greasepaint                                                             |
|  3  | A green wire stem that produces an illusory flower for 1 minute when brandished dramatically |
|  4  | A fine silk ribbon, two inches wide and nine feet long                                       |
|  5  | Bronze finger cymbals                                                                        |
|  6  | A battered top hat                                                                           |

### Using This Faction

You can use the Phantasmic Circus in your campaign to enliven an urban atmosphere. Since the Phantasmic Circus travels from city to city, the characters also might encounter the faction on the road.

The Phantasmic Circus cares more about presenting grand spectacles than following local laws. As such, the circus might create inadvertent fires or spark a panicked stampede that requires the characters' intervention. Alternatively, the circus might agree to conceal fugitives who don't seem harmful, possibly enabling the party to escape a settlement without alerting the authorities.

### Special Facility

Members of the Phantasmic Circus can add the following special facility to their Bastions.

## Circus Tent

*Level 9 Bastion Facility*

- **Prerequisite:** Membership in the Phantasmic Circus
- **Space:** Vast  [36 sq]
- **Hirelings:** 4
- **Order:** Trade

The Circus Tent is a space for musical, acrobatic, comedic, or other performances intended to delight a crowd. The tent includes seating for audience members. Your hirelings are **Performers** whose alignment matches your own. Assign names, personalities, and circus acts to them as you see fit.

### Trade: Circus Performance

When you issue the *Trade* order to this facility, the facility's hirelings turn the Circus Tent into a performance venue for 7 days. At the end of the seventh day, your portion of the proceeds is equal to 2d6 × 10 GP.

# Seers of Sea and Sky

The Seers of Sea and Sky is a faction of oracles who make predictions about the future based on movements of the clouds and waves. Open to all methods of divination, the seers encourage communication with a wide array of aerial and undersea prophets and meticulously record their prognostications.

### Goals and Activities

The Seers of Sea and Sky maintains libraries and reading rooms in many large, coastal cities. Within these hubs, seers cast Divination spells for reasonable costs; train other members in divination techniques; and maintain contact with visionaries across, above, and below the seas. Although obtaining accurate information through divination is important to the faction, sharing knowledge and imparting guidance to those most in need of it is also a crucial part of the faction's mission.

The seers primarily practice what they call hydromancy, or predictions based on watching the movement of water, and aeromancy, or predictions based on observing the weather. Members hone many subspecialties within these techniques. For example, some seers divine by scrutinizing driftwood delivered by ocean waves, while others base predictions on the movement of clouds.

#### Allies of Air and Water

Faction members live in diverse locales and include a wide array of folk. The faction's membership includes aarakocra, giant eagles, and other flying creatures who can provide detailed data about the wind and cloud movement. Other seers live beneath the waves, including bronze dragons and merfolk. Members from both groups and land-bound seers often convene, keeping the faction cohesive.

#### Truth Twice

The seers maintain a philosophy they call "truth twice." The faction doesn't consider a prophecy or revelation to be reliable unless it's been corroborated by another member—preferably one using a different Divination spell or method of divining. Checking and rechecking other members' work is routine. As a result, the seers' predictions have a reputation for reliability. The support members provide for each other contributes to the organization's close-knit culture.

![Members of the Seers of Sea and Sky perform powerful Divination magic outdoors to reveal terrible threats.](img/book/AU/055-04-013.seers-divining.webp)

### Leadership and Organization

The oracles who founded the Seers of Sea and Sky were called the Scholars of Deep Truth. As the scholars' fame spread and wealthy patrons sought their guidance, the scholars built an exclusive, opulent lighthouse to entertain petitioners. The scholars worked with undersea oracles to strengthen the accuracy of their own prophecies.

When several misleading prognostications left locals victims of suspicious seaside disasters, the scholars started questioning their sources. They soon discovered an **Aboleth** named Naurishaul was manipulating them. Before the scholars could address this shocking news, a client exposed the scholars as minions of this malevolent undersea entity. While technically true, this announcement was misleading, as the scholars hadn't realized their answers came from such a sinister source.

#### A New Beginning

The scandal could have driven the scholars into hiding or ruin, but the organization publicly acknowledged its unsavory association and resolved to do better. Members established a network of mutual education and support, teaching genuine divination methods and rigorously double-checking their revelations. They kept the remote lighthouse as the faction's headquarters but established outposts to provide answers in seaside cities where petitioners of more limited means could seek guidance.

The efforts to rebuild the scholars' reputation worked. Experts in hydromancy and aeromancy joined the faction, attracted by its openness and support. Many were diviners from far-flung places, including windswept peaks and undersea communities. To reflect the organization's growing membership and shed its tainted legacy, the faction changed its name to the Seers of Sea and Sky.

Focused entirely on their divination mission, the seers eschew hierarchy. New members are expected to respect more senior members, but the faction in general has an egalitarian outlook. Beyond the Founders (see below), members consider titles and ranks unnecessary.

#### The Founders

The faction's inner circle consists of the original Scholars of Deep Truth members, though they use that scandal-ridden name only as a cautionary tale. Other seers simply refer to them as the Founders. With a reputation for humility, insight, and rigor, the Founders are highly regarded throughout the faction.

The Founders consist of the human siblings Kessalee Cloudseeker and Narvok Cloudseeker (Medium **Mages**), haughty elf Travieste Nilvinarian (Medium **Noble Prodigy**), and gregarious gnome Bulrug Seawhistle (Small **Performer Maestro**). The Founders frequently travel to consult with faction members scattered across land, sea, and air environments.

#### Wavewatchers

Some Seers of Sea and Sky have formed a coterie called the Wavewatchers. These members enjoy watching ocean waves crash against the shore, and they spend hours in contemplation, watching light play on the waves and listening to their soothing sounds. The Wavewatchers have created a meditation trend that attracts more faction members each week. A few of the newest faction members aren't diviners at all—they're mystics or monks who've joined to seek peace from the rolling waves.

However, some faction members have fallen behind in their work in favor of wave watching, and they're producing unconfirmed divinations. This is unacceptable to the Founders, who are displeased with the Wavewatchers phenomenon. Kessalee worries that their old foe Naurishaul has infiltrated the faction once again by mesmerizing susceptible faction members.

#### Remarkable Members

Most members of the Seers of Sea and Sky are humanoids, but the Notable Seers table provides examples of unusual beings who have joined the faction.

##### Notable Seers
| Name           | Description                                                                                                                                                          |
|----------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Gnarlbeak      | Gnarlbeak, a **Giant Owl**, survived a demon attack in her youth and has become an expert on predicting fiendish incursions by studying lightning storms.            |
| Kaekylin       | The sardonic, blue-feathered **Aarakocra Aeromancer** Kaekylin is eager to discuss cloud formations.                                                                 |
| Quellishar     | An **Adult Bronze Dragon**, Quellishar can't speak and uses graceful dances to communicate truths he sees in the movements of the tides.                             |
| Tuppet Seaclaw | Tuppet, a pensive **Sea Hag**, shares insights gained by watching schools of fish migrate; she's understanding if clients are cautious about interacting with a hag. |
| Vort           | The **Water Weird** Vort uses tide pools to perform scrying rituals.                                                                                                 |

#### Joining the Seers of Sea and Sky

Anyone with an interest in studying the sea or sky to gain insights can join the faction as a scribe or assistant for the diviners who perform the bulk of the faction's work. Joining as a diviner is more difficult. Applicants must travel to the faction's lighthouse and request membership from any senior member, who makes the final admission decision. Those who don't qualify for membership are usually still welcome to stay at the lighthouse as guests.

#### Lighthouse Academy

The prior incarnation of the Seers of Sea and Sky, the Scholars of Deep Truth, renovated a sprawling lighthouse as an upscale retreat for wealthy petitioners. The lighthouse remains the seers' headquarters and the Founders' home, but it has been renovated to align to the seers' new mission.

The Lighthouse Academy, as it's now known, houses dozens. New members have built outbuildings around the lighthouse and equipped them with bunk rooms, scrying chambers, and libraries.

Few residents of the Lighthouse Academy work alone; daily mentoring and mutual confirmation of predictions keep the seers in close contact with each other. Shared living spaces here outnumber individual residences.

The seers employ lighthouse keepers who know little about divination but are experts in cleaning and operating the facility. To further repair their reputation, the Founders recently had these operators construct two smaller lighthouses on nearby promontories. The new lighthouses provide additional navigational guidance to sea travelers. Many captains in turn give seers free passage, citing the value of the lighthouses while secretly believing that no seer would seek passage on a ship that wasn't destined to reach its destination safely.

### Renown Rewards

Characters gain renown with the Seers of Sea and Sky by delivering prophecies or completing quests. As their Renown Scores increase, they gain access to various perks, as shown on the Seers of Sea and Sky Renown table. If your campaign doesn't use renown, the DM decides if and when characters earn these perks.

##### Seers of Sea and Sky Renown
| Renown Score | Reward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|:------------:|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|      3+      | You can hire sailors and dockworkers as hirelings for half the usual cost.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
|      5+      | You can purchase the costly Material components required for Divination spells you can cast from faction members.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
|      10      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 10, the seers give you an Uncommon magic item from the Rod, Staff, or Wand category.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
|      15+     | The seers arrange ship's passage for you on any seagoing voyage at no cost.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
|      20+     | Allies of the seers rescue you from drowning or shipwreck, if they can reach you.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
|      25+     | You can request the aid of 1d6 **Aarakocra Skirmishers** for one mission of your choice. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
|      30      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 30, seers share their magical insight with you. You always have the *Locate Object* spell prepared, and you can cast it once without expending a spell slot. You regain the ability to cast it in this way when you finish a Long Rest. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for this spell (choose when you gain this reward). You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. |
|      40+     | You can request for one of the seers to cast the *Divination* spell for free on your behalf. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|      50+     | Faction members openly share information with you. Additionally, you can arrange for an audience with the Founders. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

### Seers of Sea and Sky Characters

The Seers welcome anyone interested in prophecy or who lives at the boundary between the land and the sea. Fishers, sailors, and dockworkers are no less valued than diviners and oracles.

Cleric, Druid, Ranger, and Wizard (especially those with the Diviner subclass) are all good fits for this faction, as are characters with proficiency in Nature or with the Sailor or Seer Apprentice background.

#### Member Benefits

If you're a member of the Seers of Sea and Sky, you receive these benefits.

***Modest Lifestyle.*** The seers provide you with funds to support a *Modest* lifestyle (1 GP per day).

***Trinket.*** Roll on the *Seers of Sea and Sky Trinkets* table to determine what trinket you have from your time with the Seers of Sea and Sky.

#### Seers of Sea and Sky Trinket

Adventuring Gear

---

##### Seers of Sea and Sky Trinkets
| 1d6 | Trinket                                                                       |
|:---:|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | A piece of driftwood with natural whorls and gnarls that spell out your name  |
|  2  | A windsock                                                                    |
|  3  | A tide chart for an unfamiliar seashore                                       |
|  4  | A roll of seaweed with cryptic symbols written on it in octopus ink           |
|  5  | A small cog from a lighthouse's turning mechanism                             |
|  6  | Four oddly shaped white feathers that fit together to form a perfect triangle |

### Using This Faction

The Seers of Sea and Sky are an excellent source of prophecies to steer a campaign. Faction members receive guidance from the natural world and freely share their divinations rather than hoard them. If the natural world is somehow at risk—for example, threatened by aberrations or fiends—the seers might be the first to know.

The faction can also answer specific questions the characters might have, like where to find a necessary item. The seers are precise and careful with their divinations and therefore trustworthy—but deciphering their enigmatic guidance might lead the characters on new adventures.

>>*All of fate is written in the Weave, and few places on the Material Plane are closer to raw magic than the sea and sky. There is wisdom is seeking truths from these places.*— Alustriel

### Special Facility

Members of the Seers of Sea and Sky can add the following special facility to their Bastions.

## Lighthouse

*Level 9 Bastion Facility*

- **Prerequisite:** Membership in the Seers of Sea and Sky
- **Space:** Roomy  [16 sq]
- **Hirelings:** 1
- **Order:** Harvest

This tall lighthouse has rotating lenses that make its light visible from a significant distance, even in poor weather. Your hireling operates and maintains the machinery and ignites or douses the light as you direct.

### Vision Charm

Time in your lighthouse inspires you to pierce the darkness. After spending a Long Rest in your Lighthouse, you gain the Charm of Darkvision (see the *Dungeon Master's Guide*).

### Harvest: Illuminate Treasure

When you issue the *Harvest* order to this facility, you instruct the facility's hireling to watch the surrounding area for lost or discarded valuables. The hireling watches for 7 days. At the end of that time, you can hire salvagers to retrieve treasures your hireling spotted. You must spend 100 GP to hire the salvagers, who return with treasure worth 1d4 × 100 GP.

# Sheltering Hands

The Sheltering Hands is a benevolent organization of abjurers and advocates who operate emergency shelters and soup kitchens in far-flung settlements. This well-respected faction uses Abjuration magic to protect the vulnerable and remove afflictions.

### Goals and Activities

Sheltering Hands members are skilled abjurers who focus on providing services to the needy. Faction members solicit donations of food, money, or land, then share them in directed ways to reduce as much suffering as possible. They operate shelters, aid disease-stricken regions, and work to lift curses.

Beyond fulfilling immediate needs, members of the Sheltering Hands help individuals rebuild their dignity, security, and self-determination. Its members happily share bread with the hungry, but they also delight in helping the hungry set up communal farms and other enterprises so they can feed themselves. Members also work against despots to build community solidarity and create support programs that help marginalized populations voice their needs.

Abjurers of the Sheltering Hands also teach the rudiments of defensive magic to those who show interest and aptitude. Faction members who are mages lift curses and remove debilitating conditions; even members who can't cast the appropriate spells usually know enough to recognize what spells are needed.

Many of those the faction helps become members themselves, seeking to pay forward the support they've received and prevent others from suffering as they did.

#### Balance of Faith

The Sheltering Hands isn't a religious organization, though some of its members are priests and many venerate deities of mercy, charity, and self-sacrifice. The faction doesn't favor any particular religion and welcomes good-hearted members of all sorts, including former followers of evil faiths who have renounced their earlier affiliations. This nonsectarian policy allows the Sheltering Hands to coordinate relief or rescue efforts across organizations.

#### Fieldwork

Instead of expecting those in need to journey to them, small teams of Sheltering Hands members travel to deliver food, refresh supplies, and cast Abjuration spells. Spells from other schools of magic are often useful to repair structures, produce a bounty from sickly crops, or chase away opportunistic predators, so teams often include spellcasters with specialties other than Abjuration.

### Leadership and Organization

![Members of the Sheltering Hands cloak the vulnerable with protective magic.](img/book/AU/056-04-014.sheltering-hands.webp)

The faction's leadership council consists of five members. The council members make the hard decisions of allocating limited resources to do the most good.

The Leadership Council table presents the members of the faction's leadership council. All are Lawful Good.

##### Leadership Council
| Name              | Council Member                                                                                                                                        |
|-------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Elbeezar Ghont    | Elbeezar (Medium **Mage**), a human, comes from a farming village that supplies the faction's soup kitchens.                                          |
| Nylusk the Golden | Nylusk (Medium **Mage**), an orc, proudly walks with a limp earned while breaking up a riot.                                                          |
| Opener-of-Thought | Opener-of-Thought is a **Deva** who prefers the form of a placid orangutan.                                                                           |
| Slade Margid      | Slade (Medium **Archpriest**), who is a human, believes the organization should put more resources into helping communities form long-term solutions. |
| Zosh Curvin       | Zosh (Small **Mage**), a halfling, believes the organization should focus on providing emergency aid rather than pursuing long-term solutions.        |

#### Dueling Priorities

Friction within the Sheltering Hands comes from disagreements about how the organization might best help those in need. Some members believe the Sheltering Hands should focus on providing emergency aid to those experiencing hardship. Others believe that while providing aid is important, the organization should focus more on providing resources to communities to address societal struggles that cause long-term suffering. Some tension exists between these two groups, each of whose members believe their priority is superior. This dispute was minor at first, but some Sheltering Hands members have started refusing to serve alongside those of the opposite group, throwing off the faction's carefully planned administration.

#### Joining the Sheltering Hands

The Sheltering Hands welcomes all recruits interested in aiding the faction's mission, whether by offering protection during a faction outing or providing spellcasting services.

#### Andarin's Aegis

Andarin's Aegis is a large academy located in the temple district of a bustling metropolis. The Sheltering Hands established the academy as a school where its members can learn more about Abjuration magic, but, consistent with Sheltering Hands impulses, it has been used when needed as a soup kitchen, an impromptu medical center, a veteran's service center, and a shelter for children without homes. Its students are eager to learn from those who use Abjuration magic to aid others.

Andarin was one of the faction's earliest council members. Centuries after his death, many members still cite his dedication to helping the unhoused, but fewer remember his innovative contributions to the corpus of Abjuration spells.

The academy's dean is Malvanar Lumblin (Medium, Neutral Good **Aberrant Cultist**), a garrulous human who has reformed his wicked ways. Students hang on his words when he talks about the beholder cult he once served. Malvanar's expertise about the antimagic nature of beholder eyes has proven revolutionary to abjurative studies.

### Renown Rewards

Characters gain renown with the Sheltering Hands by aiding communities in great need or completing faction missions. As their Renown Scores increase, characters gain access to various perks, as shown on the Sheltering Hands Renown table. If your campaign doesn't use renown, the DM decides if and when characters earn these perks.

##### Sheltering Hands Renown
| Renown Score | Reward                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
|:------------:|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|      3+      | The Sheltering Hands provides you with simple food and lodging anywhere members are providing it to others.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
|      5+      | A Sheltering Hands member can cast *Lesser Restoration* on you or an ally in any town or city where the faction operates. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|      10      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 10, the seers give you an Uncommon magic item from the Armor or Staff category.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|      15+     | The Sheltering Hands hides and protects any magical item you want to ensure remains out of the hands of evil.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
|      20      | If you or your companions are at risk of suffering from dehydration or malnutrition while within 5 miles of a town or city where the faction operates, faction members bring you enough food and water to meet your needs.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
|      25+     | A Sheltering Hands member can cast the *Greater Restoration* spell on you or an ally in any town or city where the faction operates. This reward is free the first time you use it. On subsequent occasions, you must provide any Material components normally consumed by the spell.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
|      30      | The first time your Renown Score reaches 30, faction members teach you to magically protect those you care for. You always have the *Warding Bond* spell prepared, and you can cast it once without expending a spell slot. You regain the ability to cast it in this way when you finish a Long Rest. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for this spell (choose when you gain this reward). You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. |
|      40+     | You receive a 20 percent discount on spellcasting services purchased from faction members.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
|      50+     | Faction members openly share information with you. Additionally, you can arrange for an audience with the council members. Once you use this reward, you can't do so again until your Renown Score increases.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

>>*The selfless mission of the Sheltering Hands is close to my heart. I relish the times I can visit these friends and bolster their supportive magic with my own.*— Alustriel

### Sheltering Hands Characters

Anyone with the ability and inclination to serve others has a place in the Sheltering Hands. As members travel to places where help is most needed, stalwart warriors are just as valuable as priests and mages. Faction members work closely together, so cooperation and conviction are required.

![Members of the Sheltering Hands receive Healer's Kits to help those in need.](img/book/AU/057-04-015.healers-kits.webp)

The most welcome are Cleric, Fighter, Monk, Paladin, and Wizard (especially those with the Abjurer subclass). Characters with proficiency in Medicine or Religion, proficiency with an *Herbalism Kit*, or the Acolyte or Ward of the Sheltering Hands background are also welcome.

#### Member Benefits

If you're a member of the Sheltering Hands, you receive these benefits.

***Healer's Kits.*** The Sheltering Hands gives you a *Healer's Kit*. If you return a *Healer's Kit* with no uses remaining, the Sheltering Hands gives you a new *Healer's Kit*. Once you make this exchange, this benefit can't be used for 7 days.

***Trinket.*** Roll on the *Sheltering Hands Trinkets* table to determine what trinket you have from your time with the Sheltering Hands.

#### Sheltering Hands Trinket

Adventuring Gear

---

##### Sheltering Hands Trinkets
| 1d6 | Trinket                                                 |
|:---:|---------------------------------------------------------|
|  1  | A tin of lotion to ease rashes                          |
|  2  | A religious tract for an unfamiliar deity of healing    |
|  3  | A child's drawing of two parents smiling                |
|  4  | A utensil with a fork on one end and spoon on the other |
|  5  | A pouch of sweet spices                                 |
|  6  | A windup toy soldier holding a large shield             |

### Using This Faction

The Sheltering Hands is a benevolent organization without hidden motives or sinister secrets. It can come to the aid of the characters if they're in need. Alternatively, the faction might ask the characters for help: Perhaps a protective ritual needs a rare ingredient, or a shipment of food and medicine requires dedicated guards.

You can involve the characters in faction rivalries between those who disagree about organizational priorities. Such conflicts can be compelling because both sides have good intentions and neither is a clear villain.

### Special Facility

Members of the Sheltering Hands can add the following special facility to their Bastions.

## Soup Kitchen

*Level 5 Bastion Facility*

- **Prerequisite:** Membership in the Sheltering Hands
- **Space:** Roomy  [16 sq]
- **Hirelings:** 1
- **Order:** Recruit

This kitchen prepares large quantities of food and serves it to hungry people.

### Recruit: Kitchen Operation

When you issue the *Recruit* order to this facility, you instruct your hireling to operate the soup kitchen for 7 days. Ingredients cost you 50 GP. If you would lose a Bastion Defender or a hireling on the next roll on the *Bastion Events* table, someone who believes in your work steps into their place and takes over immediately. You don't count as losing that Bastion Defender or hireling.

### Traveling Snacks

After spending a Long Rest in your Bastion, you gain a magical Charm that lasts for 7 days or until you use it. The Charm allows you to cast *Goodberry* once without expending a spell slot.


------

# Chapter 5: Magic Items

![A retired adventurer shows his grandchild the artifact he found long ago in a dragon's lair: {@item Queen Ehlissa's Marvelous Nightingale|AU}.](img/book/AU/058-05-001.ch-5-splash.webp)

This chapter describes magic items particularly suited for settings brimming with arcane power. It also presents rules for magic item evolution, allowing you to create magic items that grow in might as your characters do, and ready-made evolving magic items meant as special rewards.

## Magic Items

The Magic Items table lists the magic items in this section and whether they require Attunement, ordered by item rarity. These items use the magic item rules in the *Dungeon Master's Guide*. The descriptions that follow are presented in alphabetical order.
